 5612: 
 5613: # _raw_whois( $query, $server ) -> string | undef
 5614: #
 5615: # Purpose:
 5616: #   Open a TCP connection to a WHOIS server on port 43, send the query,
 5617: #   and return the full response as a string.  Uses IO::Select for all
 5618: #   read timeouts so that alarm() is never needed -- alarm() is unreliable
 5619: #   on Windows and inside threaded Perl interpreters.  The connect timeout
 5620: #   is handled by IO::Socket::INET's own Timeout parameter, which uses the
 5621: #   OS-level connect(2) timeout and works on all platforms.
 5622: #
 5623: # Entry criteria:
 5624: #   $query   -- the domain name or IP address to query (defined, non-empty).
 5625: #   $server  -- the WHOIS server hostname (default: 'whois.iana.org').
 5626: #   $self->{timeout} -- seconds; used for both connect and per-read waits.
 5627: #
 5628: # Exit status:
 5629: #   Returns the raw WHOIS response string (may include CRLF line endings).
 5630: #   Returns undef if the connection fails, the write fails, or the entire
 5631: #   timeout expires before any data arrives.
 5632: #
 5633: # Side effects:
 5634: #   Opens and closes one TCP socket to port 43 on $server.
 5635: #   Writes one line ("$query\r\n") to the socket.
 5636: #   Calls _debug() with a trace line.
 5637: #
 5638: # Notes:
 5639: #   The IO::Select loop reads until the server closes the connection or
 5640: #   $self->{timeout} seconds elapse without any new data.  Because WHOIS
 5641: #   servers close the connection after sending the response, a clean EOF
 5642: #   is the normal termination; the timeout is only needed for unresponsive
 5643: #   servers.  Each call to select() resets the per-read deadline, so a
 5644: #   slow server that sends data steadily will not time out even if the
 5645: #   total response takes longer than $self->{timeout}.  This is intentional
 5646: #   and consistent with the previous alarm()-based behaviour.
 5647: sub _raw_whois {
●5648 → 5675 → 5684●5648 → 5675 → 0 5648:     my ($self, $query, $server) = @_;
 5649: 
 5650:     # Default to the IANA root WHOIS server
 5651:     $server //= 'whois.iana.org';
 5652:     $self->_debug("WHOIS $server -> $query");
 5653: 
 5654:     # Connect -- IO::Socket::INET Timeout covers the TCP handshake only
 5655:     my $sock = eval {
 5656:         IO::Socket::INET->new(
 5657:             PeerAddr => $server,
 5658:             PeerPort => $WHOIS_PORT,
 5659:             Proto    => 'tcp',
 5660:             Timeout  => $self->{timeout},
 5661:         );
 5662:     };
 5663:     return undef unless $sock;

 5800: 
 5801: =head1 ALGORITHM: DOMAIN INTELLIGENCE PIPELINE
 5802: 
 5803: For each unique non-infrastructure domain found in the email, the module
 5804: runs the following pipeline:
 5805: 
 5806:     Domain name
 5807:         |
 5808:         +-- A record  --> web hosting IP  --> RDAP --> org + abuse contact
 5809:         |
 5810:         +-- MX record --> mail server hostname --> A --> RDAP --> org + abuse
 5811:         |
 5812:         +-- NS record --> nameserver hostname  --> A --> RDAP --> org + abuse
 5813:         |
 5814:         +-- WHOIS (TLD whois server via IANA referral)
 5815:                +-- Registrar name + abuse contact
 5816:                +-- Creation date  (-> recently-registered flag if < 180 days)
 5817:                +-- Expiry date    (-> expires-soon or expired flags)
 5818: 
 5819: Domains are collected from:
 5820: 
 5821:     From:/Reply-To:/Sender:/Return-Path: headers
 5822:     DKIM-Signature: d=  (signing domain)
 5823:     List-Unsubscribe:   (ESP / bulk sender domain)
 5824:     Message-ID:         (often reveals real sending platform)
 5825:     mailto: links and bare addresses in the body
 5826: 
 5827: =head1 WHY WEB HOSTING != MAIL HOSTING != DNS HOSTING
 5828: 
 5829: A fraudster registering C<sminvestmentsupplychain.com> might:
 5830: 
 5831: =over 4
 5832: 
 5833: =item * Register the domain at GoDaddy (registrar)
 5834: 
 5835: =item * Point the NS records at Cloudflare (DNS/CDN)
 5836: 
 5837: =item * Have no web server at all (A record absent)
 5838: 
 5839: =item * Route the MX records to Google Workspace or similar
 5840: 
 5841: =back
 5842: 
 5843: Each of these parties has an abuse contact, and each can independently
 5844: take action to disrupt the spam/phishing operation.  The module reports
 5845: all of them separately.
 5846: 
 5847: =head1 RECENTLY-REGISTERED FLAG
 5848: 
 5849: Phishing domains are very commonly registered hours or days before the
 5850: spam run.  The module flags any domain whose WHOIS creation date is
 5851: less than 180 days ago with C<recently_registered =E<gt> 1>.
 5852:

 5862: 
 5863: =back
